[[Mr. Błaszczak showed it.]] With respect for the family of the deceased, I also ask for this and I extend my condolences to the family. Ladies and gentlemen! The prosecution began intensive action on Mr. Igor Stachowiak's death immediately after receiving information on this matter. She has interviewed 58 witnesses, including some on several occasions, who have received as many as 11 expert opinions, including expert opinions, which... Some of them were requested by the family of the deceased and the representatives of the family. On average every few days, the prosecution issued further resolutions in the investigation, which are intended to lead to an effective and reliable, I stress, reliable, extremely meticulous explanation of all the circumstances of Mr. Stachowiak's death. [[They haven't established anything in a year.]] She's already collected 16 volumes of investigation files and five volumes of cache files. Let us remember that the task of the prosecution in particularly complex cases, and such cases certainly belong to this criminal history, is to defend the indictment in court. All doubts, in accordance with the law in force in Poland, are and must be interpreted by the court in favour of the accused. That is why this meticulousness and accuracy so that those responsible who have committed infringements should be held liable if, of course, they have committed such an infringement. In that case, allow me to present the most important actions of the prosecution in a chronological manner. The death of Mr Stachowiak was made on 15 May 2016 at the Wrocław-Stare Miasto Police Station. Immediately after receiving information about this matter, a deputy district attorney for Wrocław-Old City appeared at the police station. Together with the police officers of the Home Office, he took action under Article. 308 Code of Criminal Procedure, it's about securing evidence of crime, possible crime. To this end, they inspected the premises of the police station and collected traces, photographing every activity and detail. They also secured and downloaded footage from a camera mounted in the so-called. The taper, which means the electric taser. The Assistant District Attorney, along with the medical examiner, examined the body of the deceased at the police station. On the spot, he interviewed more people, including a police officer from the Police Department of Prevention and Patrol KMP, in order to stop Mr. Stachowiak and use a taser against him. He secured the device, as well as the device that had - at least it had the task - to record surveillance footage at the police station. Seven police officers were examined immediately at the scene. This study did not show that they were under the influence of alcohol. On the same day, and so on the day of Mr. Stachowiak's death, the prosecution appointed an expert from the Cathedral and the Department of Judicial Medicine in Wroclaw for further examination of the body and sections and thorough histopathological and toxicological studies. They were made the next day, on May 16th. On that day, the District Attorney's Office for Wrocław-Old City formally opened an investigation into the involuntary manslaughter of Mr. Stachowiak in the police station. The investigation was entrusted with the execution of the Office of Internal Affairs of the Police Headquarters, which was set up precisely to carry out investigative activities in cases involving crimes committed by police officers. The investigation itself, which I would like to stress because of a misunderstanding, is independent of disciplinary proceedings within the police force and has been treated as an own investigation conducted by the prosecutor because it concerns police officers. The D.A. has decided that he's going to do all the important work on this case, which, of course, also affects the burden on the D.A.'s work. On the same day, the day after Mr. Stachowiak's death, two more witnesses were questioned. The next day, the prosecution secured additional recordings of telephone calls from Wrocław-Old City, and appointed an expert to determine whether the substances found at the arrested Igor Stachowiak are psychoactive drugs, whether they are, in short, drugs or boosters. I'll be back for the result of this test, which was positive. Further investigative work must have been done by experts to inspect the bodies of 10 officers. It's about police officers who suffered superficial injuries when the prisoner resisted the street, then in a police car and in a police station. One of the cops went to the hospital with a shoulder injury as a result of a kick. Further evidence was also secured, including police clothing, and further witnesses were interviewed. All these activities were carried out by the prosecutor's office in Wrocław, but already on 19 May, and that is what I want to emphasize, so four days after Mr. Stachowiak's death, the investigation was handed over to the prosecutor's office in Legnica. The idea was to ensure that it was conducted as objectively as possible outside Wrocław, where the tragedy occurred. For the same reasons, a few days later, because on 24 May, at the request of the Minister of Interior, Mr Błaszczak, who asked me personally to move the investigation outside the Wrocław Public Prosecutor's Office region, the national prosecutor appointed another prosecutor at my request, in this case the District Attorney's Office in Poznań. It happened, as I just mentioned, at the request of Minister Błaszczak, who cared, and he emphasized very strongly that the proceedings should be as impartial as possible and explain fully all the circumstances of police operations, could lead to criminal consequences later, if the police were to do so. However, the change in prosecutors' investigation did not slow down the proceedings. Between 19 May and 8 June, seven more witnesses were interviewed, and footage from street surveillance was secured at the places where Mr. Stachowiak was staying at the time of his arrest. A police car was also inspected, which was transported to the police station, with a finding of damage due to the struggle that occurred during the transport of Mr. Stachowiak. On June 14, one month after Mr. Stachowiak's death, the Department of Judicial Medicine in Wrocław gave the prosecution a report on the results of the autopsy. The experts ruled that the study did not allow a clear determination of the cause of death. They pointed out that it was most likely caused by two main factors. One is the use by Mr. Stachowiak of amphetamines and other narcotics, so-called. The other one's electrocution from the Taser. I would like to stress, ladies and gentlemen, this explanation for all those who are not familiar with the law on a daily basis, that the prosecutor, even if he had special knowledge and could determine the mechanism and causes of death, cannot do so, because the Code of Criminal Procedure in Poland, similarly is in all civilized countries, requires that in any such case an external, impartial expert, who has the so-called. special knowledge. And the prosecutor always has that kind of opinion, and he has to rely on it. It has not only the right, but also the duty to evaluate such an opinion later in terms of its substantive correctness as far as knowledge, of course, allows it. It is obliged to assess such an opinion from the point of view of its internal contradictions, the logic of the conclusions which the expert brings out, from the whole methodology of the study carried out, but cannot alone over the expert, apart from the expert or even more without the expert's opinion, determine what the causes of death were. Hence the key role of experts here, especially experts in forensic medicine. Already two days later, the prosecution appointed another expert, this time from the Department of Medical Medicine and the Department of Medical Medicine of the University of Poznań, to carry out further examinations and autopsies of the deceased. It was about finding out, among other things. using a CT scan of the type, nature and mechanism of the injuries. This was also what the family of the deceased brought forward, and this was also a proposal that the prosecutor decided to implement due to the expectations of the family of the deceased. On 20 June, Phonoscopy experts were appointed. Their aim was to recreate and write the content of the conversations recorded by the camera recording the sound and image being part of the electric taser. This recording, as I mentioned, in the presence of the prosecutor and officers of the Police Department's Internal Affairs Office, was secured and recorded at the police station on the day of Mr. Stachowiak's death, but recorded conversations were largely illegible and only the result of the work of the experts appointed by the prosecutor was the version of the recording that the media released a few days ago, and you saw. It was only this result of expert work that allowed to recreate the actual course that was recorded by this taser. The victim's family received this video in mid-October last year, as requested. On June 20, the prosecution appointed experts to swab DNA from Mr. Stachowiak's clothing. They found two plastic bags of white substance in his sock. In June, experts were appointed to inspect the surveillance recording device at the police station, as well as the recordings of Mr. Stachowiak's arrest recorded on witnesses' mobile phones. Further witnesses were also interviewed, and a request was made to exempt doctors from medical confidentiality who were assisting Mr Stachowiak. The next month, in July, the prosecution questioned 22 witnesses, i.e. witnesses were questioned almost every day. She also appointed further experts, and they were to analyze fingerprint lines, among others. on a telescopic bat that he had on him. The aim was also to make DNA swabs from policemen's clothing to determine whether they match the swabs from Mr. Stachowiak's clothing. By the end of last year, the D.A.'s office had interviewed more than 20 other witnesses besides the ones I already mentioned. Some of these interrogations, as I said, were repeated in order to clarify the information received. The D.A. has conducted an investigative experiment, analyzing policemen's notebooks. However, this period of investigation was determined to wait for the results of expert expertise. As I said, the expert opinion was and is crucial in this regard. However, the prosecution has no influence, as I would like to point out, on the working time of experts, but in this case they acted relatively quickly. The waiting time was significantly shorter than the national average, which we obtained on the basis of information from individual regional prosecutors, which is about 6 months. In this case, three opinions of various independent institutes, forensic experts, have been received in turn over less than a year, which also shows the intensity of the work of experts, who, I understand, have treated this matter as a priority under the weight of the case and the arguments of the prosecutor and, in any event, have carried out their duties intensively. And so, among other things. On 16 August, the prosecution received a judicial and medical opinion on the investigation of officers who participated in the arrest of Mr Stachowiak. On 31 August, policemen's clothing was examined. On 21 September, an opinion was prepared concerning among others. Cell phone and video recorder recordings at the precinct. This opinion did not show that the recorder, which did not make any records, was intentionally damaged. The reason was the bad technical condition of the disk, which was simply not suitable for use. The results of the second autopsy of the deceased came in on October 26th. There were no fractures or sprains, only bruises, epithelial abrasions and minor petechiae. They may have been caused, for example, by a struggle or a fall. Again, they considered that the study did not give rise to a clear determination of the direct cause of death. They found amphetamines and tramadol in the body of the deceased. The other substance is an opioid painkiller, so it works like morphine, in any case it's similar to morphine or other drugs. It can cause seizures, increase in blood pressure. At times, obtaining some expert opinions led to the need to order further witnesses, as well as to the questioning of further witnesses, which resulted from the conclusions of the experts' opinions. It affected the time of the case. In November, for example, an expert in the field of techniques and intervention tactics was established in order to clarify whether the use of coercive measures against Mr Stachowiak was justified. This expert took part in an investigative experiment on the market in Wrocław and in a police station designed to recreate the course of events. He stated - and this opinion was received by the prosecutor in January - that, although there were certain irregularities which he pointed out in the police's action in terms of professional action, there was no reason to accept, according to the expert, that the coercion measures applied to the detained had caused his death. According to the expert, there's no evidence that he was beaten, that is, wronged, and kicked by policemen. Therefore, in mid-January the prosecution appointed another team of experts, this time from the Department and the Department of Medical Medicine of the Medical University in Łódź, to issue a key opinion, as it seems, whether the cause of death stopped was the use of an electric stun gun against him and whether the death is related to the intervention of police officers. This opinion was received by the prosecution at the end of March, about two months ago. The experts found that Mr. Stachowiak died of cardiopulmonary failure after taking psychoactive drugs with a high probability - these are the conclusions - that he died of cardiopulmonary failure. They described his condition as an exalted delirium. It's about the disorder of consciousness combined with confusion, the lack of distinction between reality and hallucination. At the same time, it is accompanied by unnatural excitement, excitement. They considered that neither the stun guns used by the police were responsible for their death, nor the use of a taser. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u152	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the request of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club to express a vote of distrust towards the Minister of National Defence, Antoni Macierewicz. 2015 found Poland practically disarmed, with a residual, lacking staff and hardware army, organizational chaos at the highest levels of command, lack of real plans in case of war or armed conflict in Europe. Rebuilding the defence capabilities of the Polish Armed Forces in the context of the budgetary possibilities of the state is a process requiring not only huge money, but above all time, assuming the existence of political determination for such action. Such determination is not lacking in the current Minister of National Defence, Mr Antoni Macierewicz. The Polish Armed Forces came out of the stagnation in which they remained for many years. A number of actions have been taken to improve the level of security of Poland in the field of defence. These include three fundamental actions. The first is connected with Poland's strongly articulated openness to increase its commitment to NATO military alliance and to deepen cooperation with the United States in this area, the second concerns structural and personnel changes in the Polish Armed Forces, while the third is the development and consolidation of the Polish arms industry, including strengthening its role as a provider of modern solutions for the Polish army. The appointment of the Territorial Defence Army is one of the priorities of the Minister of National Defence, which will allow for strengthening the security of Poland, especially in local terms. The Territorial Defence Army is a modern and well-trained type of Armed Forces, whose creation is part of building allied defence and deterrent capabilities. The ongoing modernization of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland and the increase of allies' commitment strengthens not only the national potential, but also the entire North Atlantic Alliance. I fully share the idea that the construction of the Territorial Defence Army is in the current situation the only relatively fast, cheap and effective way to rebuild our military security. During the peace, the Territorial Defence Army will be used to conduct anti-crisis and anti-terrorist activities. The establishment of the Territorial Defence Army is also a response to social needs. The reconstruction, support by the Government of the Republic and Minister Antoni Macierewicz of the Polish arms industry deserve great recognition. Modern defence industry providing constant supplies to the Polish army using state-of-the-art technologies, developing production also for the civilian market and providing jobs for qualified professionals will be one of the main driving forces of the Polish economy. The new defence concept of the Republic of Poland, which is prepared by the Ministry of National Defence, announces a change in the system of command and control of the Armed Forces. Today the Polish Army is not only a functioning and well-managed structure and modern equipment, but also a solid foundation of patriotism for the whole society, which is based on values written in gold letters on the banners of military units: God, honor, homeland. In our opinion, and most importantly, in the general opinion of Polish Army soldiers, Mr Antoni Macierewicz is the best minister of national defence in the history of the Third Republic. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u170	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The most important thing for Poles is security and justice, which is why we are determined to heal the Polish justice system. Therefore, we are obliged to improve the functioning of Polish courts. There is a great social expectation about such changes. We owe it to the Poles. As Law and Justice, we promised a profound reform of the electoral campaign. We confirmed this promise after the election, and we keep that commitment. On behalf of the applicants, I have the honour to present a draft law amending the Law on the system of common courts and certain other laws. We brought the project on the basis of an art. Article 118 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland and on the basis of Article. Article 32 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. The proposed law is an important part of the reform of the Polish justice system. At the same time, he's part of it. The Sejm has so far adopted regulations concerning the directors of the courts, the National School of Judicial Affairs and the Public Prosecutor's Office and a comprehensive revision of the provisions of the administrative procedure. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights is working on amendments to the laws on the National Judicial Council, as well as on bailiffs and executions. In the coming months, further projects will be presented, including broadly expected, on improving civil and criminal proceedings. The reform is therefore comprehensive. As a parliament, we are responsible for changes in the law in force. These include the system, the organisation of courts and judicial procedures. The government and courts will be responsible for implementing the reform, making appropriate organisational and personnel changes. The judges themselves will be responsible for the practice in courtrooms. We trust that together we will reform the justice system, that it will work more justly and more efficiently, that all Poles will feel a good change in the courts. The amendment of the Law on the System of General Courts and of certain other laws provides for the implementation of several essential objectives, and contains several dozen proposals for amendments to implement these objectives. We want to improve the functioning of the courts so that they act quickly, effectively, more transparently, and the judgments handed down by the judges inspire citizens' confidence. Contrary to the fears sometimes expressed, the proposed changes make the judges independent of the pressure of their superiors and thus strengthen their independence. Thanks to them, cases will be conducted in a fairer way and will also affect the reduction of the pathology of Polish courts, which is the chronicity of proceedings. The project contains a number of detailed proposals for changes. Allow me, ladies and gentlemen, to refer now to the most important of them. A full draw of cases. This is to end the opaque practice of assigning cases to judges, a practice which sometimes took the form of manual control of cases by court presidents and sending e.g. politically involved judges for political matters. The principle of equal treatment of judges in cases. At present, it is not only equal, which leads to unfair division of labour in courts, but also contributes to the long-term nature of proceedings. An electronic system based on German, which is designed to weigh the quality of individual cases and to lead to the situation that, in principle, none of the judges in Poland will have more responsibilities than his colleague from the department or court. Indistinguishability of the formation of the adjudicator. This solution is intended to lead to the elimination of the pathology of long-term chronicity by an unnecessary change of composition. Once drawn, the court should not change until the case is concluded. Today, in many courts, a new judge in the department is assigned cases that have already been initiated by other judges, to the detriment of the efficiency of the proceedings. Of course, the project provides for a derogation from this principle, but these are specific cases, e.g. When the judge gets sick. Come on, limit the power of the presidents to the transfer of the judges. We're introducing strict rules for transferring a judge to another department. Today, such a transfer, e.g. A civilian to the criminal department, may be a form of harassment. The proposed regulation restricts the power of the President of the Court by regulating the manner and criteria that must be followed in such a decision. In principle, the judge's consent to such a transfer will be required. Come on, change the model of appointing the Presidents of the courts. The Minister of Justice shall be responsible for the administration of the courts and, as responsible, should be able to shape such administration in a realistic manner. The strengthening of the competence of the Minister of Justice is intended to enable rapid action and prevent socially shocking situations where prestigious functions are performed by people who have lost confidence, as in the case of a case involving suspicion of fraud of at least PLN 10 million from the Court of Appeal in Krakow. The Minister of Justice is obliged to ensure the proper functioning of the general judiciary, and the solutions so far have been inefficient and sometimes have made administrative supervision of the Minister of Fictitious Justice. The practice so far shows that the judicial community has often been guided by fellow Members' sympathies in the opinion of candidates for presidents and vice-presidents of courts, and the substantive considerations and competences have not been relevant to the support received. In some courts, as the judges themselves say, this has led to social or even Byzantine systems of connection. The amendment of the law makes the judges independent of such a system and introduces the principle that the competence of the candidate will be decisive, not the agreements or the level of the court in which they decide. Clarify the rules for the declaration of property by the judges. The statements will be extended to include precise information on all sources from which the judges derive their income, together with amounts. This is to prevent, among other things. where additional income is not included in the asset statement as it has been issued by making a declaration. The project also provides for the disclosure of complex claims of property. The introduction of clear and substantive principles of promotion. We want to eliminate the impact of social agreements or even coteries on the case of judges' promotions. We therefore propose that the mechanism for periodic evaluation of judges be abolished. The periodic evaluations introduced by the previous coalition of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party were In fact, protested by numerous legal environments as a tool of pressure on the judge and restricting his independence. The amendments are also intended to refer to the appointment of a judge of the Court of Appeal. The professional experience gained in the course of the service, rather than the level of the judiciary or the prosecutor's office at which it has been achieved, should be decisive for the appointment of such a position. Today, the promotion path completely depends on the judges of the regional courts, who recognize more than 90% of the cases, on the decisions of the superiors in the district court, which threatens their impartiality and is part of the pressure. To reduce the practice of going to rest of the sick - in quotes: sick - young judges who miraculously recover when they are retired. There will be no more automation. At the same time, we introduce the institution of rehabilitation leave of the judge as a period during which it will be possible to restore the full capacity of the judge to perform his duties. The project presented today contains a number of other solutions to improve the functioning of the courts. The transition to a system of dynamic court management, the introduction of annual assessments and information submitted by the Presidents of the courts at all levels and the possibility of drawing attention to irregularities in administrative activities is intended to allow the President to react on an ongoing basis to the negligence of the Presidents in the proceedings of the court and to realise the real impact of judges on the organisation of the work by the President. The extension of the scope of the possibility to use the institution to draw the attention of the judge performing the function of administrative activities will allow inter alia. to put an end to the abuse of their power by those judges towards the judges ruling. The system of visitation and evaluation of court activities is to be more rational. Today, the courts have a staff of visiting judges - a total of over 400 visiting judges across the country - with a rigidly defined visitation calendar, once every four years in a department, who devote time to official work rather than judgment. We propose that visits be managed where potential problems are observed, so that controls are spot and less predictable, while at the same time that some judges who are involved in the evaluation of other judges are more able to rule. This would have a positive effect on reducing delays in court proceedings. Enabling the acquisition of the right to retire by judges of women of age corresponding to the general retirement age, or 60 years. Further, the project aims to increase the efficiency of the use of funds collected in the context of monetary benefits ordered from offenders in the justice fund. This is intended to allow for a much wider use of funds from criminals than is currently the case. It is about broadening the scope for assistance to victims, reacting to the arguments raised so far for insufficient assistance, and using these resources to prevent crime. Introduce the institutions of coordinators for international cooperation, European law and human rights in civil and criminal matters in order to improve the exchange of information and experience on international cooperation and human rights. Delegation of judges to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Chancellery of the President of the Republic of Poland. The Ministry of Justice appreciates the experience and competence of judges in legislative support. It would be worthwhile for the Polish state to benefit from this kind of support if needed. The bill was submitted to the Sejm on April 12th, so more than a month ago. He's already relatively well known. Most of the proposals are welcomed, particularly those concerning the improvement and transparency of the functioning of the courts or the strengthening of the independence of judges from the Presidents of the courts. Some of the doubts raised - I hope - have been clarified so far, in particular the false accusation about the alleged creation of the possibility of the Minister of Justice's influence on judicial decisions. If the High Sejm welcomes this project, as I believe, in the coming weeks we will have further substantive work on this project in committee. I invite you to engage in a constructive discussion between your fellow Members, the legal environment, including judges, and the public. It would be a shame to lose this time for a non-essential discussion of the alleged vile intentions of political opponents. It would also be a shame to waste time multiplying arguments about the unconstitutionality of proposals, not because they cannot or should not be asked about their compliance with the basic law, but because there is no inflation of such charges, which we have been experiencing dramatically in recent months. If any dispute were to be a constitutional dispute, there would be no need for a parliament. All problems would be solved by constitutional judges for us. It would also be wrong if our Polish discussions were to be a pretext for inciting international opinion and seeking allies abroad at all costs. The experience of the judicial systems of Western countries can be a valuable enrichment of discussions, but they are extremely diverse, resulting from native traditions and conditions. Without a fundamental analysis, and in addition when they are instrumentalized, they often lead to wrong conclusions and proposals. In any case, they cannot exempt us from looking for the best solutions for Polish courts. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! We propose reform of the justice system, and today changes in the Law on the system of common courts, because this is the general expectation of the Polish society. We are determined to heal our justice system. We are committed to improving the functioning of our courts. That is why we are also open to discussion and changes that make this project better. As representatives of Law and Justice, we promised this in the election campaign, but you, ladies and gentlemen, have also announced this to all political options, so we all owe it to the Poles. Let's keep that commitment. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u177	I would like to ask the floor of Mrs Barbara Dolniak. You're welcome, my fellow Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u178	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment to the Law on the System of General Courts was introduced to the Sejm as a parliamentary project, although the statements of the representatives of the Ministry of Justice indicate that it is a concept of judicial reform developed and gradually introduced by the Ministry. The Ministry's representatives aren't even hiding it. This is done to avoid the stage of public consultation and the participation of citizens in decision-making is guaranteed by the Constitution. The new appointment of the Presidents provided for in the project is a departure from the standards set by the previous case law of the Constitutional Tribunal and constitutes a violation of the constitutional principle of separation and independence of the judiciary. The Court considers that, in order to maintain the principle of the division and balance of the authorities, it is necessary to equip the representative bodies of the judiciary with the right of binding opposition to a candidate presented by the Minister of Justice, the role of such an authority may be played by both the competent authority of the judiciary and the KRS, where the majority of the votes, in accordance with the Constitution, have judges, provided, of course, that this will not be a choice under the current PiS project. The present proposal is clearly in breach of the principle of three divisions of authorities, and the whole of the powers of appointment of governors are placed in the hands of a representative of the executive authority. According to the project, the role of the judiciary is limited to getting acquainted with the ministerial candidacy. It is worth noting that the Minister of Justice did not have such far-reaching powers even before 1989, since it was compulsory at that time to consult the college of the court concerned without being bound. Even such a small limitation of the Minister's authority in this regard is not foreseen in the present proposal. This fosters the creation of a system in which functional judges can be very available to the minister, because the maintenance of their positions will depend on the will of one man. Such a appointment of the Presidents, as well as the possibility of entrusting the functions of President in a given court to another court, including a lower order, may lead to difficulties in managing and promoting the dependence of the Presidents on the Minister, which may consequently result in the application of pressure on judges in individual cases and endanger the very important guarantee of judicial independence, the institutional independence of the courts. In creating a change in the law on the general court system, designers should bear in mind that the independence of judges cannot exist without the independence of the courts. What distinguishes a democratic state from a totalitarian state is the state of balance of the authorities and guarantees ensuring not only judicial independence, but also the independence of the courts. The institutional submission of courts to executive authorities reduces the level of public confidence in the independence of judges and consequently to the judiciary. It is negative to assess the amendment of the rules on the dismissal of the Presidents of the courts. A new condition has been added as a basis for the President's dismissal: particularly low efficiency in administrative supervision or organisation of work in a court or lower courts. After all, the assessment of the degree of efficiency is subjective and gives an unlimited authority to the decision-making authority based on this premise. There is therefore a serious risk that the presidents will spin the surveillance spiral in order to avoid the charge of low efficiency. The expiry of the term of office of the Presidents within 6 months should also be particularly negative. The term of office shall be laid down in the Act, which shall also indicate the reasons for their revocation. The proposed solution is another attempt to influence the independence of the courts and to express a misconception that personal changes themselves can lead to some positive results. You are not proposing solutions that will facilitate the improvement of justice work and allow wide access to court by reducing court fees, promoting mediation or reducing the type of cases that the courts consider. You're not talking about increasing the civil factor in the justice system, you're scaring the courts and judges, and in the meantime, you're introducing human resources laws. In the example of Wrocław, you can clearly see how the law you introduced works, such as the law concerning the police or the prosecution's office, and there was to be a good change. We submit as a modern motion to reject this bill at first reading. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u182	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! Ladies and gentlemen, although not very many people present in the Parliament's Chamber at a time when we are starting a rather important and fundamental draft law on the system of courts! I'll start by thinking, Mr. Marshal, High Izbo, of a general nature. Many in recent days, weeks, we are talking about the need for reform, the need to improve the functioning of justice, the judiciary. But I must admit, ministers, that with this healing of the work of the courts, so expected, it is in your performance a bit as with the treatment of hand pain by amputation of this hand. It is undoubtedly right to diagnose the need for change, but will giving all supervision of the functioning of the judiciary, from the stage of the judicial school and the prosecution, through the courts, to the National Judicial Council, in the hands of the Minister of Justice, achieve something? I dare have very serious - and I think it is not just me - fears that this will of course bring very rapid and desirable results from the Minister's perspective, but from the perspective of those who are so counting on these changes in the judiciary, because it is true that there are a lot of those who have great regret for the decisions that have taken place recently in the courts, unfortunately not. I think over time, the number of people who are disappointed in these kinds of changes will start to grow. To discuss in detail the draft law amending the Law on the system of common courts and certain other laws contained in print No 1491, I would like to draw attention on behalf of the PSL Members' Club. This project assumes comprehensive changes in the judicial system in Poland. These changes concern inter alia. a model for the appointment of Presidents and Vice-Presidents of the Courts, tools for external and internal supervision of the administrative activities of the courts, changes to the model of visits to the departments of the General Courts or rules for the submission of declarations of property by the Judges. How's it going today? Today, the Presidents of the Appeals and District Courts are appointed by the Minister of Justice after consulting the General Assembly of these Courts. The governors of the regional courts shall appoint the Presidents of the appeal courts after consulting the judges of the regional court concerned and the President of the district court superior. The draft provides for the withdrawal from this model of appointment of the Presidents of the Appeals and District Courts after obtaining the opinion of the General Assembly and, in the case of their negative opinion, after obtaining a positive opinion of the National Courts of Appeal and District Courts. According to the justification of the project, in the current state of law, the National Court of Justice may block the possibility of a minister's candidate being appointed as the President of the Appeal or District Court. At the same time, in order to strengthen the external supervision exercised by the Minister of Justice, the proposal provides for a derogation from the principle of the appointment of the President of the Regional Court by the President of the Appeals Court by means of a procedure for the opinion of the candidate for appointment by the Minister of Justice. As stated in the explanatory memorandum, these changes are aimed at creating a new model for the appointment of the Presidents of the General Courts by increasing the freedom of the Minister of Justice as the body responsible for ensuring the proper functioning of the universal judiciary on an administrative basis. At the same time, the Minister of Justice, after his appointment, will present the appointed presidents to the competent authorities of the judiciary, i.e. general assemblies. Changes were also envisaged in the dismissal of the Presidents of the courts. Today, the Presidents of the Appeals and District Courts may be dismissed by the Minister of Justice during his term of office in the event of a flagrant failure to fulfil his duties, and where the continuation of his duties for other reasons cannot be reconciled with the good of the judiciary. The draft assumes that the Presidents of the Appeals, District and District Courts may be dismissed by the Minister of Justice during his term of office in the event of gross or persistent failure to fulfil his duties, where the continuation of his duties for other reasons cannot be reconciled with the good of the judiciary, or the finding of particularly low efficiency of the administrative supervision or organisation of work in the courts or courts of the lower courts and the resignation of his duties. The scope of the statements of property made by judges is also widened, and the obligation to make declarations is also to be extended to the directors of the courts. The amendments are also to be introduced in the rules governing the appointment of a judge of the Court of Appeal. In the opinion of the authors of the project, it is not justified to condition that the judge has taken up this position earlier in office, e.g. Judge of the District Court, Military District Court or Prosecutor of the District Attorney's Office. The project also concerns the introduction of new tools for external and internal supervision of the administrative activities of the courts, the introduction as a rule of the system of random allocation of cases to judges and the principle of equal burden in the courts of cases. Random assignment of cases is intended to limit the powers of the Presidents of the courts to assign cases to judges and will help to achieve an equal burden on judges by working. High Izbo! In the first words of the justification, the authors of the project say that the aim is to increase the influence of the Minister of Justice on the cast of posts. And it's hard not to get the impression that that's really what this project is all about. From now on, the Minister will be able to fill any position of President of the General Court in Poland with a person who will enjoy his trust. This is not enough, but there are numerous mechanisms for disciplinary action for judges. The project assumes, among other things, that the grounds for appeal of the President of the Court will be the particularly low efficiency of administrative supervision and/or organisation of work in a court or lower courts. High Izbo! It is so generally formulated that it is equally possible to write: the Minister may at any time dismiss the President of the Court. So it is not only the appointment but also the dismissal of the Presidents of the Courts that is to be facilitated so that the Minister can - this is the impression - choose and discipline these disobedient judges. I don't know if that's the intention, Minister, but after reading the provisions of the bill, unfortunately it's hard to resist such an impression. In accordance with the proposed provisions, the Minister will have the power to appoint arbitrary, single-members and dismiss the Presidents of the Appeals Courts, the Presidents of the District Courts and the Presidents of the District Courts, and at the same time completely deprive the judiciary of its influence on these decisions. High Izbo! The strengthening of the role of the Minister of Justice in the administrative supervision of courts as a result of the project undermines the constitutionally guaranteed separation of judicial authority. It's also a certain cost you're putting yourself at. On the one hand, you can see no purpose for which this law would lead, and again you are criticizing, the interest of organizations that monitor the state of the judiciary in the world and thus in our country. The solutions provided for in this draft also seriously restrict the principle of judicial independence and violate fundamental human rights such as the right to court, the right to protection of privacy and the right to protection of personal data. That's how the world interprets interference in the independence of the courts. This, too, must be seen as being interpreted in this way. This project is another very big step, unfortunately, towards the liquidation of the independence of the judiciary in Poland. And to remind you - I think - it is worth it, although it already rained here today, from this speaker, that on 9 May the case we are talking about, projects, ideas for a new [[♪ The bell ♪]] the shape of the judiciary has been the subject of an interim review of the human rights of the UN States. We don't need this. In summary, Mr. Marshal, High Izbo, the Polish People's Party club does not see the possibility of supporting the project in this form. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u185	Thank you. And Mr Ireneus will gain. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u186	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the proposal for a parliamentary bill amending the Law on the system of common courts and certain other laws. The draft law submitted is the most extensive amendment in this term of office to the law governing the general judicial system. It should be added that the long-awaited amendment, taking into account the announcements of judicial reform announced in public by representatives of the Ministry of Justice for months, is also expected because the changes in the functioning of the justice system in Poland, and in particular in the functioning of the general judiciary, are very much needed. This is clearly demonstrated by the results of the examination of the level of confidence in the institutions of the State in which the courts are at one of the last places. For example, according to a study presented on 7 March 2016 at a conference held in the Supreme Court, more than 80% The respondents agreed that there was excessive bureaucracy in the courts and that the courts were slow. In the view that the courts are primarily concerned with their own interests, 15% agreed of the subjects, and rather this was the response of a further 45% , which gives a negative result of 60% surveyed. In view of the significant scope of the changes proposed in the draft submitted, which cannot be discussed within 5 minutes of the presentation, I will address the most important issues. First, the Act introduces the principle of random assignment of cases to judges and the principle of equal burden on judges in cases in order to ensure an equal and fair burden on the work of judges and to guarantee the impartiality of the parties to proceedings. This change should be assessed positively. Currently, the requirement of random allocation applies only in criminal proceedings, and to a very limited extent. The other cases are assigned by the head of departments, and this is not always an assignment that ensures full transparency. Needless to say, the system allows abuse and does not build citizens' confidence in justice. The proposal was welcomed by many judges, in particular regional judges, who were often very burdened with work. The principle of random allocation of cases guarantees the parties access to the competent court. The practical implementation of this principle by using a special allocation algorithm within the ICT system does not raise any objections. Such a solution guarantees that there is no possibility to influence the lottery. Secondly, the draft law adds an article. 47b, which introduces the principle of the invariability of the court. This change should also be assessed positively. At the moment, the unchangeable composition of the court ruling in civil proceedings is not regulated by law, and the practice in courts is subject to the orders of the Presidents of the courts or of the heads of departments who decide to assign cases to judges. As in the case of lots of cases, exceptions have also been introduced from this principle, in cases such as cases such as: sickness or transfer of the judge. Thirdly, it is worth focusing on a change that was most often commented on in the media, namely a change in the model of appointment of the presidents and vice-presidents of the courts. The proposal contained in the proposal assumes that the Minister of Justice will appoint the Presidents of the Appeals and District Courts without having to obtain an opinion on the appointments for these posts. At present, such an opinion shall be delivered by the General Assembly of the Judges of Appeal in the case of a candidate for President of the Appeal Court or a General Assembly of the District Judges in the case of a candidate for President of the District Court. In the event of a negative opinion of the said authorities, a positive opinion of the National Judicial Board is required. In the event of a negative opinion, the candidate designated by the Minister of Justice shall be blocked. In addition, the amendment provides for the appointment by the Minister of the Regional Courts and not, as is currently the case, by the competent President of the Court of Appeal. It should be noted that emergency situations, in this case the problem of the disease of the Polish justice system, require extraordinary measures, which are proposed in the draft law in question, but in this case the proposed amendments are in our view going too far, given the ruling of the Constitutional Court of 9 November 1993 K 11/93, according to which the participation of the Minister of Justice as administrator of all courts in the appointment of the Presidents of the courts may be important but cannot be dominant. It is also debatable whether the Minister of Justice should be [[♪ The bell ♪]] At the same time, additionally burdened with the appointment of regional court presidents, of which there are as many as 321. That is why, in our view, we refer to the article. 4 of the Constitution, the optimal solution would be to elect the Presidents of the courts in general elections from among candidates - judges who meet the conditions set out in the Act. My time is up. The Free and Solidarni Circle requests that the bill be submitted for further legislative work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u223	Thank you very much. The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Mr Łukasz Piebiak, Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice, will now speak. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u224	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Of course, referring to your questions is the role of the rapporteur, Mr Wróblewski, and I will not worry about this role, but I would like to address a few points on behalf of the government, but I would like to stress that the position of the Council of Ministers has not yet been prepared with regard to this project, which is being discussed today, but, of course, the Ministry of Justice, as the appropriate resort, has a certain view on this legislative proposal and this is a positive view. The draft submitted represents a broad and multi-faceted revision of the rules governing the functioning of the general judiciary. It is aimed at improving the efficiency of the administration of courts in the administrative area and at improving the efficiency of court proceedings in the course of the investigation of cases. The most important proposals from many of the proposed changes are aimed at ensuring that the Minister of Justice has a full, real impact on the performance of administrative judicial oversight tasks, and this entails the responsibility of the Minister of Justice within the Council of Ministers for the area of judicial administration not covered by the principle of judicial independence. The proposal, as has already been said many times today, provides for the freedom of the Minister of Justice to fill the positions of the Presidents of the courts, while maintaining the restrictions on their dismissal. This objective is also implemented by the proposed amendments concerning annual information on the activities of appeal courts, their assessment and the possibility of increasing or reducing the amount of the functional allowance in extreme cases, i.e. distinguishing or negative assessment of the outcome of the annual performance of the tasks entrusted to the Presidents of the courts. Importantly, the proposed law provides for a novel in the form of an obligation to draw up annual information on the activities of courts also by the Presidents of regional and district courts, which will be subject to assessment by the Presidents of the superior courts. The above-mentioned real sense of responsibility of the Presidents for the functioning of the courts will give importance to annual information as a real expression of the achievements of the judicial administration and will have an incentive effect on the performance of the tasks entrusted. Notwithstanding the above, the bill provides for a number of changes concerning the functioning of the judicial system in order to improve its judicial action. Particular attention should be paid to the principle of random allocation of cases, ultimately by using IT solutions, and this serves to eliminate the unequal and discretionary allocation of cases and to strengthen - and not weaken - the principle of judicial independence. At the same time, this should ensure that the parties are more confident about the case in accordance with the standard set out in Article. 45 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, i.e. by an impartial and independent court. The solution also correlates with the proposal to introduce as a principle the continuous composition of the court and the obligation for judges to conclude cases that have already been opened, regardless of the delegation or the change of office, and this will in turn contribute to the efficiency of the procedure and the elimination of the situation of unjustified transfer of cases to other judges or formations of the court. Here you have drawn attention and criticised this solution, that this project does not improve, improve the conduct of proceedings. That's not true. It is precisely this principle of invariability of the formation of the court that prevents cases from starting from the beginning to the clear fury of the parties to the proceedings which are affected by such consequences of changing the formation of the court. Another thing is the introduction of the institutions of the co-ordinators. international cooperation and human rights in civil and criminal matters or the creation of European Judicial Network contact points in courts. This should again help to facilitate the access of judges to information concerning the law, case law or practices of foreign states and international courts and tribunals and, consequently, speed up these proceedings, the most difficult procedures of dealing with a foreign element. It is also important to respond to the expectations of the judge by introducing age changes to enable judges to turn to rest. The proposed solutions provide for equalisation of the age to be enjoyed by judges of women with retirement age resulting from the general social security system of 60 years. We also have here changes aimed at introducing an institution of rehabilitation leave for judges. He will make it possible to regain his capacity for service in case of long-term illness, while at the same time guaranteeing the elimination of cases of retirement by judges who, after a long period of treatment or rehabilitation, recover their ability to work, but are not obliged to provide it, while being able to benefit for life from rest and at the same time take up other employment. This issue also raises understandable negative social feelings and is also in the media. That's not an option for today. This law introduces the possibility of a judge returning to judgment when his health is improved. We also have a number of further positive developments here, such as the possibility of a regional court being promoted directly to an appeal court in the event of a sufficiently long judicial period. And there's nothing wrong with that, there's no jump or assassination. This is the introduction of some elementary justice into the system. It can't be like today, for example. a judge of the district court, a lawyer or legal counsel may apply for the post of judge of the Supreme Court, and the same judge of the district court may not apply for a position in a lower court, but not for an authorization such as an appeal court. We take into account here, as the project shows, a certain collective judicial internship, not a level structure of the judiciary. This is actually restoring some rationality in the structure of the legal system. We continue to introduce the possibility for judges to work on a 50% educational leave allocation of cases. This is a matter of pro-family policy also in the judiciary. No one has done so yet, and it would be beneficial for the judiciary, and for judges, most often mothers, so that they can return to judgment, but not at full load, especially in large cities, where this burden is gigantic and absolutely incompatible with the obligations of raising a small child. It is also important to make the possibility of transferring judicial references more flexible to other work places. In fact, the proposal is very broad and concerns a few more issues, but this is not the time or place to present it here in detail, especially since the rapporteur has told us about it. I will refer to a few more votes in the discussion. There was talk of expropriation in the field of IT programs. Why is this happening? First of all, it's not expropriation, and if it's expropriation, it's due compensation, because no one is exempt from the obligation to maintain constitutional standards. Therefore, this option, the final option of course, must be. As of today, we have a terrible situation in terms of the computerization of the judiciary. We took work at the Ministry of Justice, doing some things, as we estimate, 10 times cheaper than the same things were done by our predecessors, even at extremely low quality. But the process of building IT programs is ongoing, this is a long-term process. It cannot be that at a time when maintenance contracts or such situations are over, the contractor operating under market conditions puts the Minister of Justice, and in fact the Treasury, taxpayers, against the wall and says: Now pay us 10 times as much, and if not, we will shut down your systems and prevent you from functioning. The defense of today's state of affairs is really a defence of smartness, an act contrary to the interests of the Treasury and all taxpayers. It will not be that the Minister of Justice will abuse this tool, but the Minister of Justice must have this tool in order not to be put in a position of no choice: either to pay as much as anyone asks, or that someone will lead to the fact that e.g. At some point there will be court records, National Court Registers or perpetual books, because someone will want to make a lot of money. Mr Kropiwnicki, presenting the Platform's position, mentioned the discretionary additions that this was scandalous. It's about the administration, the president, the vice president. Well, there is nothing outrageous about it, and what is more, if you get to know the matter well, then you will find out whether everyone who thinks in the same way that to this day the additions for all: for the presidents, for the vice presidents, etc., have a fork structure. Therefore, the Minister of Justice already has this discretion today. And they're not really related to any kind of circumstances. The fact that the Minister of Justice internally makes the amount of allowances dependent on e.g. the size of the courts, the number of judges subject to the President of the Court of Appeal, district or district, these are the Minister's internal regulations, which can be applied today, they do not need to be applied tomorrow, and we are introducing clear and clear rules here. You're good at administrative work - we can give you a reward, but not for good judgment, which is in the party business, but for good administration, because it's a question of an allowance for administrative activities. You're not doing well - we can call you off, but we can also lower your supplement and say: improve, organize a better job in this court because we don't want to immediately take advantage of the possibility of staff exchanges. What we're doing today is really civilizing, covering the legal matter of all these issues that are completely discretionary today. And if you knew about today's legal situation, you'd know it. Unless you know and you won't admit it. Mrs Gasiuk-Pihowicz's allegations that the Minister of Justice will decide where, to whom, what case, and in this way will decide how they will end, are, of course, a misunderstanding, because this law is breaking up with such practices. [[Member Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz: No, by his governor he will be able.]] Of course, there is an on-call judge, a Member, who is and must always be, for example. Night shifts because there are responsibilities for temporary arrest. It's for situations like this. I would recommend reading the Rules of Procedure of the General Courts, a description of the situations in which judicial services are used. Just like that, in extraordinary ways, not to assign a case to a chosen judge. You say that judges can be discriminated against, somehow affected by the whip because they are being relocated. Then read about how it is today, Member. Read about what it's like today. Today they can be transferred between departments without permission, and this is done by the President of the District Court, and also by appeal after consulting the College. [[Mr Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz: After consulting you.]] It does not say that, in general, the transfer within the court takes place with consent. The transfer with the consent of the court is only included in this project. So this project limits today's freedom of the courts to transfer, it only limits it to certain situations when no one wants to be moved and someone needs to be moved. Today we have full freedom here. Who's doing this? Of course, the president. [[Member Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz: Collegiate.]] You can say, the president will be the minister's man. What's going on next? There's an appeal. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u246	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Maritime Fisheries contained in print No 1541. The first reading of the draft law on the amendment of the Law on Maritime Fisheries from print No 1404 took place at the 39th meeting of the Sejm on 6 April this year. The project was then addressed to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation for consideration. The Commission at its meeting on 11 May this year. it considered this draft law and presented a report. The proposal concerns the limitation of the right to transfer individual fishing quotas between shipowners and the waiver of collective fishing quotas. During the work on the project, amendments were tabled to clarify the provisions of the draft law and the legislative and editorial nature. Amendments of substance have also been tabled. One of them was intended to introduce a record of the master of a fishing vessel having a total length of less than 8 m from the obligation to enter in the monthly catch report the quantities and species caught or caught by marine organisms. According to § Paragraph 7 1 of the Regulation of the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development of 22 September 2015 on the detailed allocation of overall fishing quotas and additional fishing quotas for shipowners of fishing vessels of less than 8 m overall length fulfilling the conditions set out in the above mentioned Regulation. the provision may lead to catches of species of marine organisms subject to the overall quota without determining the amount of the fishing quota of those species, entered, of course, in special fishing permits authorising fishing in the waters of the territorial sea, sub-areas 24 and 26 of the Baltic Sea, Gulf of Gdansk and Gulf of Puck. In view of the wording of the above provision and the fact that catches of the abovementioned provisions are concerned. vessels do not count in overall catch quotas, it is not intended to require that the quantities of species caught or caught by masters of fishing vessels of less than 8 metres in overall length be entered in the monthly catch report. The obligation to report catches in fishing logbooks applies only to fishing vessels of a total length of 8 metres and therefore catches of fishing vessels below that length do not count against the overall catch quotas. The operators of fishing vessels less than 8 m should complete monthly catch reports only to the extent that they can identify the fishing areas, the type and number of fishing gear used, the duration of the catches and the dates and hours of landings and the identification of the fishing vessel, its master and the month in which the report was drawn up. The imposition of this obligation on masters of fishing vessels below 8 m is currently not justified. Another amendment related to the reduction of tax charges for licences issued to shipowners of vessels engaged in recreational fishing activities. This amendment was forced to verify the amount of licence fees granted to shipowners of vessels engaged in an economic activity involving the transport of anglers to selected marine areas for recreational fishing. The tax charges currently in force for these licences are too high and have led to a poor economic situation for ship owners. The reduction of tax charges will improve the situation of these shipowners and may broaden the range of persons interested in carrying out such activities. The Commission accepted all the amendments tabled. As there were amendments of the Legislative Bureau which had ordered the text of the bill, the committee also accepted them. There was also a discussion with the fishing community, questions were asked by ministers and directors of the department, who had analyses that justified changes in the law. I would therefore ask the High House to accept the report of the Committee in print No 1541.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u247	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u248	Yes. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project was discussed, in the parliament hall, but we will also return to some arguments, because it is impossible not to tell about them. The most important amendment to the proposed bill is the abolition of the possibility to transfer individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels. The aim of the proposed regulation is to create a barrier that will prevent the sale of individual fishing quotas. In the assessment of part of the fishing environment, the sale of quotas granted in special fishing permits leads to a preference for shipowners who are able to pay a certain amount of money to the vessel owner from whom the individual fishing quota is to be obtained. Shipowners who do not have adequate financial facilities are very often automatically excluded from the possibility of obtaining an additional fishing quota to increase the profitability of their business. Individual fishing quotas or fishing days which shipowners are unable to use in accordance with the provisions in force should be returned to the Minister responsible for fisheries, who would allocate them. The allocation of additional fishing quotas is a tool for a fair distribution of returned fishing opportunities among shipowners of fishing vessels who are interested in fishing to a greater extent. The project assumes that an individual fishing quota or number of fishing days may be transferred in full or in part to another fishing vessel of the same vessel which, for at least 24 consecutive months before the date of submission of the request for transfer, was the vessel owner of the fishing vessels involved in that transfer. The proposed law also extends the period for the vessel owner to withdraw in a given calendar year from all or part of the quota granted to him in a special fishing permit, as I said, to the species of marine organisms or fishing days by 15 July. Another major change proposed in the proposal is the repeal of the rules on aggregate fishing quotas. The solution for creating the aggregate fishing quota has not yet been used. The condition for the creation of a aggregate quota is the waiver by shipowners of fishing vessels associated with the recognised fish producer organisation concerned of their individual fishing quotas for the species of marine organisms concerned so that the sum of those quotas does not exceed 5% the total fishing quota of this species allocated to Poland for the calendar year concerned. By-catch of marine organisms which have been extracted under the Maritime Fisheries Act has been exhausted will also be prohibited. The aim of this solution is to achieve more effective conservation of marine living resources, which fishermen often ask for. In accordance with the simplification project, it is also intended to be subject to an international exchange which can be carried out either of its own motion or at the request of the operator of the fishing vessel, which is intended to increase the number of operators who will be able to submit an appropriate application. Proposal to repeal the article. The 35th Law on the organisation of the fish market is due to the fact that the system currently used by fish producers' organisations for reporting the first sale fulfils all the conditions of Regulation No 1224/2009 and therefore no additional system is needed. The draft law provides in transitional provisions that cases concerning the transfer of an individual fishing quota or fishing days and the exchange of international fishing quotas on request, initiated and not completed by a final decision, until the date of entry into force of this Act, shall apply to the existing provisions. The Law and Justice Club supports the draft as set out in the committee. After discussion and exchange of views with fishermen, there is an opportunity after a certain period of operation of this law, if it were to enter into force, also to correct it, as is usually the case in life, while the arguments presented by fishermen concerned mostly the adoption of this law. However, we are also concerned about the reservations of some environmental representatives and we are also open to discussion. As of today, Law and Justice, the Law and Justice Club is in favour of this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u250	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! High Izbo! As I mentioned in the first reading discussion, the main objective of this project is to eliminate the negative phenomenon of the transfer of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels. As we know, the overall fishing quota allocated to Poland in accordance with European Union rules is divided into individual quotas which limit the size of fishing carried out by shipowners of fishing vessels. In the current legal state, ship owners who are unable to catch the quota allocated to them for a given year shall have the right to transfer it to other shipowners with greater fishing opportunities. According to many fishing communities, this solution has negative consequences in terms of quota trading. The result is the creation of a group of shipowners whose main source of income is not from fishing but from the sale of quotas allocated to them. On the other hand, shipowners who do not have adequate financial capacity are excluded from the possibility of obtaining an additional fishing quota, which deprives them of an opportunity to increase the profitability of their activities. The project's authors propose a far-reaching reduction in the transferability of these amounts. This will only be possible between vessels belonging to the same ship owner for at least 24 months. The remaining amounts are to be returned to the Minister responsible for fisheries, who will make another allocation. High Seym! The direction proposed by the authors of the bill seems appropriate. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u256	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Kukiz club15 on the government proposal for a law amending the Maritime Fisheries Act. After consulting the fishing industry, I can say that the proposed law takes into account the interests of Polish fisheries operators. The most important change planned is to prevent the transfer of individual fishing quotas between fishing vessels. Today, there are certain situations of transfers between ship owners. This gives rise to a privileged situation for shipowners who are able to pay certain money for a given fishing quota. On the other hand, some shipowners started to profit from the sales of quotas allocated to them instead of fishing. If the ship owner cannot use the individual amounts, he shall return them to the Minister's disposal and in this way they shall be distributed. It is good that an extension of the time limit for the vessel owner to submit an application for resignation in a given year is provided for in part or in whole of the individual fishing quota or fishing days allocated to it by 15 July. To date, such an application had to be submitted by 30 June. By extending this period, individual fishing quotas or fishing days available to shipowners can be better estimated so that the resignation concerns only the part which the shipowners will not be able to use until the end of the year. After consulting industry representatives, I can say with all responsibility that the bill is a response to the existing paradoxes. I am glad that at last the Polish government listened to the voice of fishermen. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u268	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At its 39th meeting on 21 April 2017, the Senate adopted a resolution on the submission to the Sejm of a draft law amending the Law on the Prohibition of the Promotion of Communism or other totalitarian system by the names of buildings, objects and public utility devices. The previous law, of 1 April 2016, on the prohibition of promoting communism, introduced a ban on the promotion of communism or other totalitarian system by local government units through the names of buildings, objects and public utility facilities, including roads, streets, bridges and squares, which commemorate or promote persons, organisations, events or dates symbolising the totalitarian system. Outside the scope of the Act, the names remain, inter alia, schools, hospitals, cultural institutions, settlement names and monuments glorifying the totalitarian system. Leaving the names of institutions and monuments commemorating events and people having a criminal influence on the history of Poland gives the supporters of totalitarianism the opportunity to promote their views and has a harmful effect on society. It is therefore necessary to amend the law by creating legal instruments for removing demoralizing monuments and names from public space. This will send a clear signal to the public that the State is implementing constitutional rules prohibiting the totalitarian system, and condemns and condemns all activities related to the promotion of totalitarian attitudes. The current project envisages a ban on the construction of monuments promoting the totalitarian system. The concept of monuments is also intended to include mounds, obelisks, columns, sculptures, statues, busts, souvenir stones, plates and plaques, inscriptions and signs. The provision provides for exemptions covering monuments which are not publicised, those which are exhibited for purposes other than the promotion of the totalitarian system, as part of artistic, educational, collector's, scientific or similar activities, and monuments entered in the register of monuments. These exemptions are intended to preserve monuments which do not serve to promote the totalitarian system. Another is the exclusion from the regime of monuments located in cemeteries and other resting places. These are places of memory and rest of war victims, both soldiers and civilians. The preservation of the memory of these victims has a moral dimension, so the amendment does not interfere with such monuments. The procedural protection of the application of the law is the granting of competence to voivods in the removal of monuments promoting the totalitarian system. Voivodeships will issue decisions ordering the removal of forbidden monuments. As in the case of street names, the decision will be subject to a positive opinion of the Institute of National Remembrance. The appeal authority in these matters shall be the Minister responsible for Culture and National Heritage. In the event of failure to comply with the decision ordering the removal of the monument, the provisions of the Law of 17 June 1966 on enforcement proceedings in the administration will apply. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 5c added to the Law on the Prohibition of the Promotion of Communism was provided with a provision giving rise to the repeal of the resolution of local government units on the erection of a monument which is incompatible with the provisions of the Act. The Act contains two adaptations. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 there is a provision introducing an annual deadline for the elimination of prohibited names of institutions and municipalities' support units. In the event of failure to do so, the same procedure as that provided for in Article will apply. 6 Act on the prohibition of the promotion of communism procedure for the removal of illegal names of objects. In the case of monuments which will exist on the date of entry into force of the Act, the owner or user of the perpetual land on which the monument is located will have 12 months to restore the lawful state. If this obligation is fulfilled, the Treasury will bear the costs of removing the monument, unless the monument has been erected with the assistance of the owner or the perpetual user. If the obligation to remove the monument is not fulfilled, the voivode will issue a decision on the removal order and the property entitled will lose the right to reimburse the removal costs of the monument. The Act does not contain provisions allowing for the repeal of resolutions underpinning the monument before the date of the Act's entry into force, as such resolutions, when they are implemented, i.e. As soon as the monument is erected, they expire from the legal system. The establishment of a 3-month period of vacathio legis is intended to enable all addressees to prepare for new regulations. By representing the Senate in the work on the project, I propose that the High House adopt this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-24-sejm-42-1.u282	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I have the honour and honour to present the position of our club on the subject of the Senate bill amending the Act on the Prohibition of the Promotion of Communism or other totalitarian system by the names of buildings, objects and devices of general interest.
